# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

Reviewed HEAD: `bf436c6e93c3eef9e115159ede951a5ecaee5472`, 2026-11-03. Immediate before/after comparison: `574097f` → `bf436c6`; accumulated repair review: `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD`. Specification: the user's round-4 brief, original approval bar, [m1](../m1/REPORT.md), [m2b](../m2b/REPORT.md), [m3](../m3/REPORT.md), and `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.

**Approval remains blocked.** The exact named m3 blockers are fixed, but the new shortest-span rule can bind a qualifier to the wrong drug and map the refused product's originator. The six-token limit also admits a qualified multi-token drug alias that the previous version refused. Separately, the original single-token qualifier pass still prevents a refusal from reaching its drug. These are current-database map and QA reproductions, not malformed-data fixtures or external pharmacology assumptions.

## Specification blockers

### M4-B1 — P1, new regression: equal-length binding refuses the wrong drug

Exact unchanged-server request:

```text
POST /api/explore
{"text":"osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab"}
```

HTTP 200 contains:

```json
{"center":{"id":"CHEMBL1201583","label":"BEVACIZUMAB","trials":122},
 "also":[{"refused":"osimertinib with generic version"}]}
```

This is an excerpt of the full response in [attack_http.json](attack_http.json). With the exact previous linker and previous `_JOINERS`, the centre is **OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410** and the refused span is **generic version of bevacizumab**. The current full-string resolver returns `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no drug ID. Reversing the drugs (`bevacizumab with generic version of osimertinib`) also selects the qualified second drug. Adding just `a` — `osimertinib with a generic version of bevacizumab` — changes the current result back to OSIMERTINIB. This is a distance/tie artifact, not a product-identity distinction.

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Previous linker's first evidence | Current HTTP first evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `NCT03909334\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL3353410\|NCT03909334` | `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab?` | `ORG:astrazeneca\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL3353410\|` | `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

Both current answers have 12 evidence rows; status/evidence-count checks alone miss the subject switch. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact before/after QA](names_final_checks.json).

**Cause:** `with` was removed from `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:91-95`). `_drug_spans` supplies the first drug before the second (`222-239`). Both `osimertinib with generic version` and `generic version of bevacizumab` are four-token candidate spans. The shortest-span selector iterates drug spans in that order and updates only on strictly shorter length (`255-272`), so the first candidate wins. Its qualifier tokens enter `out`; the correct second candidate is then disallowed by `span & out` (`264`). BEVACIZUMAB remains acceptable at `181-189`, and the map takes it at `src/app.py:88-95`.

Additional m3 controls `osimertinib; generic version of bevacizumab` and `osimertinib， generic version of bevacizumab` change from a whole-input refusal to a BEVACIZUMAB centre. Their separators are not clause boundaries, so the shortest-span algorithm consumes the left drug and leaves the qualified right drug available. The previous over-refusal was a usability failure; the new originator leak is a blocking regression. [Exact comparisons](names_separator_checks.json).

**Required outcome:** bind the qualifier to the product it describes without consuming a separately named valid drug. This input must never map or answer for generic bevacizumab as BEVACIZUMAB. Preserve the repaired albumin-bound paclitaxel case; globally reinstating `with` as a separator would reopen m3-B1.

### M4-B2 — P1: six-token cap admits qualified longer aliases and distant qualifiers

`POST /api/explore {"text":"generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid"}` now draws **PACLITAXEL POLIGLUMEX / CHEMBL2108628**, with `also:[]`. The same full input is `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` at `/api/resolve`; previous `574097f` refuses with no centre. This exact string was already a m3 supplementary control. [Full current HTTP](attack_http.json), [exact previous/current comparison](names_attack.json).

**Cause:** the stored alias occupies five tokens. `_drug_spans` deliberately retains that full non-overlapping name (`src/query.py:222-239`), but `_qualified_drug_spans` only examines spans up to **six tokens** (`258`: `range(b - a, 7)`). The qualifier plus `of` plus the full alias requires eight. No tested span can contain both `generic version` and the complete drug, so nothing is refused. The map then accepts the bare full alias inside the refused product description. This is a new regression even though the bare poliglumex alias correctly remains supported.

The cap also leaves a simpler inherited bypass: `generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab` (seven tokens) draws BEVACIZUMAB with `also:[]`; the six-token control `generic version of the antibody bevacizumab` refuses. QA `Which trials evaluate generic version of the monoclonal antibody bevacizumab?` returns 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows. `What targets does generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid act on?` also returns current evidence for CHEMBL2108628 (12 rows, first ACTS_ON edge), despite the qualified product being unresolved. [HTTP responses](attack_http.json).

The **plain qualified phrase** is a new map/QA regression: previous refusal/0 evidence becomes a centre/7 profile-mechanism rows. The longer `What targets ... act on?` question already bypassed the previous linker (12→12 rows), as did `Which trials evaluate generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid?` (4→4). Those prefixed-question failures are inherited. [Exact before/after checks](names_final_checks.json), [plain-phrase comparison](names_attack.json).

**Required outcome:** an arbitrary token-distance ceiling must not turn a known qualified product into a positive originator match. Preserve valid bare multi-token aliases and fail safely when qualification cannot be bound reliably.

### M4-B3 — P1, inherited: the single-token prepass blocks its own extension to the drug

| Exact `/api/explore` text | Current result | Current `/api/ask` reproduction |
|---|---|---|
| `biosimilar version of bevacizumab` | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583; only `biosimilar version` refused | `Which trials evaluate biosimilar version of bevacizumab?` → 12 BEVACIZUMAB trial rows |
| `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463; only `liposomal formulation` refused | `Which trials evaluate liposomal formulation of doxorubicin?` → 3 DOXORUBICIN trial rows |

The existing resolver refuses both complete products. Both parent leaks also occur with `574097f`, so these are **inherited**, not attributed to the latest repair. They nevertheless defeat the approval bar and the new claim that refusal is bound to its drug. [Current HTTP](attack_http.json), [before/after](names_attack.json).

**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-151` marks the qualifier and immediate neighbour before binding. `_drug_spans(tokens, refused)` skips those tokens (`252`, `225-229`), and every candidate crossing them is rejected by `span & refused` (`264`). Thus the helper cannot extend a previously recognized qualifier across `version of` or `formulation of` to its drug. These examples fit within six tokens; raising only the cap does not fix them.

The two-product input `liposomal formulation of doxorubicin and biosimilar version of bevacizumab` links **both parent drugs**, selects DOXORUBICIN and puts BEVACIZUMAB in `also`. By contrast, adjacent `liposomal doxorubicin and biosimilar bevacizumab` correctly refuses both. [Two-product controls](names_attack.json). The m3 hyphenated control `paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles` also still leaks PACLITAXEL: its one-token qualifier cannot extend across the pre-refused neighbour `with`. The unhyphenated fixed example is therefore not a general solution. [Exact prior control](names_new_boundaries.json).

**Required outcome:** single-token and multi-token qualification need one consistent binding process; already refused qualifier tokens must not make their own qualified drug available as a positive hit.

## No-relink and question-answering assessment

The refusal blockers above reproduce even when matched spans may be relinked in an otherwise identical HEAD linker. They are not caused by the no-relink repair.

- All **7 actual demo questions**, sent over HTTP, have complete response objects identical to the original `bfcb83d` query module. They are also identical to immediate predecessor `574097f` in the focused QA comparison. [HTTP demo evidence](demo_results.json), [focused comparison](names_attack.json).
- Across **2,584 unique multi-token alias keys**, 402 entity lists change; none changes the first accepted identity or introduces a new Drug. The common change removes an embedded parent Drug/company name. Bare salt/full-name identity is preserved. [Alias comparison](names_attack.json).
- The isolated counterfactual uses HEAD's exact helpers, dictionaries and data, and changes only `(refused | matched)` back to `refused` in the overlap guard (`src/query.py:168`). **1,648 questions**: 28 completely identical, 1,596 entity-metadata-only changes, 24 semantic changes, 0 exceptions. It covers the **400 alias keys changed specifically by this toggle × four QA templates, plus 48 company/target/mixed controls**. This differs from the 402 aliases changed by the full previous-to-current repair comparison above. [Reproducer](qa_relink_sweep.py), [all inputs and changed answers](qa_relink_sweep.json).
- The 24 semantic changes remove a Company read from inside a stored full Drug alias, switching `company_portfolio` to `drug_profile`. For example `Tell me about GSK 1120212` previously used nested GSK and returned 12 GlaxoSmithKline portfolio rows; current returns TRAMETINIB's three profile/mechanism rows. `Tell me about EPOETIN BETA` stops treating BETA as company `ORG:beta`. These changes agree with the stored full aliases; they are not adverse regressions found by this review.
- Explicit `What trials evaluate erlotinib hydrochloride and erlotinib?` still links both separately named records. Multi-entity target/company questions and mixed accepted/refused inputs were checked separately; passing demo/salt checks do not excuse the subject-switch failures above.

**Conclusion for this isolated change:** no adverse no-relink QA regression was found in the exercised corpus. This is a bounded result, not proof for every possible sentence.

## Should-fix items and remaining successful checks

1. **Inherited over-refusal of a neighbouring target/company.** `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` refuse the entire input; the reversed forms retain EGFR/AstraZeneca. The generic-version equivalents now retain the target/company. This follows the unconditional immediate-neighbour rule at `src/query.py:149-151`; it does not identify the neighbour's type or syntactic role. A `with` between the target/company and qualifier also preserves them. No invented edge is needed for this usability failure. [Order comparisons](names_attack.json), [HTTP with controls](attack_http.json).
2. **Unchanged qualifications:** unsupported lowercase IDs/Investigator names, raw malformed-request errors, empty-form retention and numeric Infinity are marked NOT FIXED above. None is treated as a new repair regression or an invalid numeric value actually drawn.
3. **Stored graph guarantees hold in the checked snapshot.** Seven retired nodes, 142 retired edges, zero live edges with retired endpoints, no self-loops. All six node types are covered. PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES, ACTS_ON and role-aware SPONSORS match their endpoints. Trial details, sponsor count/class, Drug global count/max phase and actions match stored values. [Exhaustive evidence](db_validate.json), [snapshot facts](db_snapshot_facts.json).
4. **Chunking and size:** the 1,001-Drug fixture crosses three count batches with count/weight 1 for every item. Disease MONDO_0005233 returns 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 72.117 ms** in the recorded call. **24 concurrent requests / 12 workers**, all expected 200 shapes, **598.259 ms** total. These are local samples, not load benchmarks. [Fixtures](db_fixtures.json), [HTTP](http_results.json).
5. **No writes or model calls on exercised application paths.** Explore issues reads; `Engine.ask` explicitly uses `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). The app does not globally open SQLite read-only, so this is a path/integrity observation rather than a database-permission guarantee.

No remediation was applied.

REPORT m5
# Independent relation-map review — round 5

Reviewed HEAD: `514929c83fb7ab8cdd23fdd50eb300bd409c3250`, 2026-11-03. Immediate predecessor: `bf436c6`; cumulative repair comparison: `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD`. Specification: the user's round-5 brief, original approval bar, [m1](../m1/REPORT.md), [m2b](../m2b/REPORT.md), [m3](../m3/REPORT.md), [m4](../m4/REPORT.md), and `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.

**Approval remains blocked by two independently reproduced product-refusal bypasses.** An attached separator defeats a suffix refusal; a listing word can detach a formulation qualifier from its sole drug. Both draw the refused product's originator and return that originator's QA evidence. Exact predecessor comparisons show both are inherited bypasses, newly identified here, rather than newly introduced by this commit. All previously named blockers are FIXED for their exact inputs under the revised fail-closed policy.

The new implementation also merges drug aliases across its own comma/semicolon boundaries. This can select the wrong salt or suppress a valid drug alone in another item. Those findings, a lone-alias rejection, and input cost are separated from the decisive product-refusal blockers below. Ordinary over-refusal of drugs sharing one item is **accepted behavior**, not a finding.

## Verification, evidence and limits

- Requested suite: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` — **326 passed in 16.03 s**, exit 0. Bytecode disabled; test temporary files confined to m5. [Output](pytest.txt), [environment/hashes](pytest_summary.json).
- Reused previous scripts by copying/adapting them into m5. Replayed **62 original name cases**, 14 additional probes, 13 prior mixed-name regressions, 30 m3 supplemental controls, all 45 m4 attacks, 37 varied QA inputs and 2,584 multi-token alias keys. The old 1,648-question no-relink isolation sweep also completed with 0 exceptions. [Execution manifest](names_replay_execution.json), [name coverage and dispositions](names_findings.md). ## Blocking findings

### M5-B1 — P1: attached separator hides a known refused suffix

Exact unchanged-server request:

```json
{"text":"bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, OSIMERTINIB only in `also`, and **no refusal**. The linker reports `matched_text:'bevacizumab-awwb/'`, `matched_alias:'Bevacizumab awwb'`.

The identical input at `/api/resolve` yields `bevacizumab-awwb: BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` and `osimertinib: EXACT / CHEMBL3353410`. Bare `bevacizumab-awwb` is refused. Thus the test names a product the repository itself refuses; no external pharmacology assumption is needed. [Actual HTTP responses](m5_http.json), [exact current/predecessor linkage](names_m5_confirm.json).

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Current evidence |
|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib?` | 12 rows, first `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib?` | 12 rows, first `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

**Cause:** the splitter at `src/query.py:128-130` leaves `/` attached to the word; listing symbols are recognized only as whole tokens (`145-149`). `RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX` is anchored to the end of the string (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213-223`), so the extra slash defeats it. No contiguous *word* run removes that slash. Alias matching then calls `canonical_key`, which discards punctuation (`src/textrules.py:339-346`; `src/query.py:182-197`), recovering the originator alias after the guard missed it. `Engine.explore` trusts that hit (`src/app.py:88-95`).

`bevacizumab - awwb/ osimertinib` and `bevacizumab-awwb| osimertinib` also leak over HTTP. With spaces on both sides of `/`, the guard recognizes the suffix and HEAD refuses the entire item; semicolon or `and` correctly preserves only osimertinib. The whole-item slash refusal is accepted under the user's rule, not counted as over-refusal. Exact `bf436c6` comparison reproduces the attached-slash leak: **inherited, not introduced by HEAD**.

**Required outcome:** punctuation that alias normalization removes must not hide a refusal. Never map or answer for CHEMBL1201583 from this refused component. Retaining the separately recognized osimertinib or refusing an ambiguous combined item can both be safe outcomes; selecting BEVACIZUMAB cannot.

### M5-B2 — P1: a listing word inside one product description detaches its qualifier

Exact input **`liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin`** returns HTTP 200 with **DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463**, **3 trials**, and `also:[{"refused":"liposomal"}]`. The full-string resolver returns a single **BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT** component with no accepted drug. `and` joins descriptions of one formulation here; it does not introduce another named drug. [HTTP](m5_http.json), [independent reproduction](ui_boundary_probe.json).

`Which trials evaluate liposomal and nanoparticle formulations of doxorubicin?` returns **3 originator trial rows**, beginning `NCT01970540|EVALUATES|CHEMBL53463|NCT01970540`. The singular QA version independently yields the same three rows. The singular/plural map forms and `liposomal and sustained release doxorubicin` also fail. [Actual QA](m5_http.json), [independent singular QA](ui_boundary_probe.json).

**Cause:** `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:94-95`) and the unconditional boundary at `146-149` isolate `liposomal` before examining the drug. The scan at `156-164` only considers words within one resulting item; nothing in the second item matches the current refusal patterns, so DOXORUBICIN remains a positive hit (`190-198`). The full product predicate recognizes the original phrase via `liposomal` (`src/textrules.py:148,207-208`). The fail-closed rule cannot protect a qualifier the splitter has already detached.

This is a different mechanism from the attached-punctuation bypass. It also occurs with the exact previous linker, so it is **inherited**, now confirmed against the changed design. Ordinary `liposomal doxorubicin and osimertinib` is not the requested failure case: separate named products should retain their isolation. No over-refusal of a valid same-item drug is alleged here.

**Required outcome:** a conjunction inside a single qualified product must not make its originator acceptable. If such a phrase cannot be resolved safely, refuse its drug. Preserve the successful separate-drug mixed-input controls.

## Should-fix findings, separate from the approval blockers

### M5-S1 — P2: alias matching crosses the very list boundaries used for refusal

Two complementary effects share a cause:

1. **A valid drug alone in its own item disappears.** `biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex` returns only `refused:'biosimilar anastrozole'`. Its second item is a valid standalone alias: `Arimidex` and `biosimilar anastrozole and Arimidex` both select **ANASTROZOLE / CHEMBL1399**. The resolver gives a refused first component and `Arimidex: ALIAS / CHEMBL1399`; the exact predecessor retains Arimidex. Current comma QA has **0** evidence versus standalone Arimidex's **2** rows. `biosimilar Prolia, xgeva` similarly loses the separately listed Xgeva, while Xgeva alone returns DENOSUMAB / CHEMBL1237023 and **3** trial rows. These are actual CLI HTTP reproductions. [HTTP](cross_item_http.json), [prior/current comparison](names_m5_confirm.json), [extra controls](names_cross_item_refusal.json).
2. **A different salt identity is assembled from two items.** `Erlotinib, hydrochloride biosimilar bevacizumab` now selects **ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742**, **31 trials**, while declaring the second item refused. The exact predecessor and current full resolver preserve first-item **ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553**, **118 trials**. The semicolon form repeats the wrong-salt selection; the `and` form preserves the parent. Trial QA returns 12 salt rows, first `NCT00445848|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1079742|NCT00445848`. This is a constructed list-contract test, not a claim that hydrochloride bevacizumab is a marketed product. [HTTP](m5_http.json), [comparison](names_m5_confirm.json).

`_drug_spans` receives only listing-word positions to skip (`src/query.py:155`); it has no clause/segment boundaries (`231-248`). Aggressive keys join `anastrozole Arimidex` into the stored `anastrozole (arimidex)` alias, or `Erlotinib hydrochloride` across a comma. Line **165** assigns the entire span using only `seg[a]`. A qualifier in its first item refuses tokens in the second; a qualifier in its second item misses the span assigned to the first. The final positive matcher also joins windows without segment checks (`174-198`). This is **new versus bf436c6** for the cited inputs.

The Arimidex/Xgeva cases satisfy the user's explicit should-fix exception: the valid alias is **alone in its own item**. They are not ordinary intended same-item over-refusals. Keep both refusal discovery and accepted-name matching within the same item boundaries; do not borrow words from a refused item to build another identity.

### M5-S2 — P2: an intrinsic qualifier rejects a valid lone stored alias

**`Pegylated interferon alfa-2a`** is a unique stored ChEMBL synonym of **CHEMBL1201560 / PEGINTERFERON ALFA-2A**. Used alone, HEAD refuses it. The canonical name `PEGINTERFERON ALFA-2A` and exact node ID both draw that live drug. The previous linker reached the same drug through residual `alfa-2a`, accompanied by an incomplete refusal; HEAD now produces no map. Of 7,511 unambiguous alias keys, this is the only newly refused previously linked lone alias. [Stored alias/comparison](names_m5_summary.json), [HTTP controls](m5_http.json).

The full resolver already blocks the synonym via `pegylated`, so this is a **shared dictionary/guard inconsistency**, not a new resolver disagreement. The alias names the stored pegylated drug itself; no originator substitution is demonstrated. It is should-fix under the explicit lone-valid-alias test, and does not block a demo. Do not generalize this into accepting every known alias before product guards: the B1 suffix alias shows why such an exemption would be unsafe. Relevant logic: `src/textrules.py:148,207-208`; `src/query.py:153-168`.

### M5-S3 — P2: all-substring refusal scan creates substantial input cost under the global lock

For an unqualified item containing n tokens, `src/query.py:161-163` tries n(n+1)/2 word runs. Constructing and scanning their strings incurs cubic aggregate character work for equal-length tokens. The scan runs even without any drug. Instrumented exact-predecessor/current runs use the same inputs/data; times are local observations, not universal performance guarantees. [Reproducer](perf_probe.py), [results](perf_results.json).

| Exact constructed input | Characters | bf436c6 linker | HEAD linker |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `" ".join(["x"] * 50)` | 99 | 0.0012 s | 0.0220 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 100)` | 199 | 0.0022 s | 0.1332 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 200)` | 399 | 0.0046 s | 0.9778 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 250)` | 499 | 0.0064 s | 1.7907 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 500)` — direct API | 999 | 0.0116 s | 18.3512 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 1000)` — direct API | 1,999 | 0.0249 s | worker exceeded 20 s; stopped |

At 250 tokens HEAD makes **31,625 calls to each predicate**, processing **10,479,750 aggregate characters** across the two predicates, versus 250 calls each / 500 characters in the predecessor. A single 500-character word takes 0.0073 s; 250 semicolon-separated x items take 0.0102 s; a late biosimilar qualifier short-circuits at 0.0225 s. This is token/run growth, not simply request byte length.

Uninstrumented HTTP confirmation: the allowed 499-character input took **1.8472 s** in `/api/explore`, **2.2104 s** in `/api/ask`; adding a valid osimertinib within 499 characters took **2.1657 s**. Four simultaneous allowed inputs completed in **7.9315 s**, with individual latencies up to **7.9215 s**. A normal EGFR request dispatched 0.1 s after one long lookup took **1.8568 s**, versus **0.0366 s** after it completed. The actual server holds `engine.lock` during linking (`src/app.py:247-263`). A 501-character text is promptly rejected, as intended. [Raw HTTP measurements](perf_http.json).

The 500-character text cap does not bound every caller: `/api/check` allows a 4,000-character answer (`src/app.py:260`), which is relinked by `check_groundedness` (`src/query.py:605-608`). With `{"text":"EGFR","answer":" ".join(["x"]*500)}`, the 999-character answer exceeded a **5-second** client timeout; the following ordinary EGFR map exceeded its **2-second** timeout while GET `/` still succeeded. The owned process was then terminated. These are bounded observations, **not a completed latency or permanent-deadlock claim**. [Exact payload/results](perf_check_http.json), [cleanup](perf_check_lifecycle.json).

Preserve refusal coverage while avoiding exhaustive reconstruction of every word run, and cover the answer-field caller when bounding work. This is should-fix performance evidence, separate from originator leaks.

## Demos, mixed inputs and other successful checks

- All **36 page examples** render successfully: 13 resolver, 7 QA, 9 prose-check, 7 structured-check. All 42 demo/map-example callbacks pass. **35/36 complete demo payloads equal m4**; the sole difference is the Mvasi refusal span expanding to the whole item. Its intent, answer, checks and empty evidence remain unchanged. No demo is unexpectedly blocked. [Comparison](ui_demo_compare.json), [responses](ui_demos_responses.json), [click/render tests](ui_m3_checks.json).
- Seven actual HTTP QA demos retain evidence counts **12, 12, 11, 1, 12, 0, 0**. Six complete responses equal bfcb83d; Mvasi differs only in refusal metadata. [HTTP comparisons](http_demo_results.json). Passing demos do not excuse the mixed-input subject errors above.
- `Which trials evaluate osimertinib and biosimilar bevacizumab?` retains 12 osimertinib rows; the corresponding sponsor question starts AstraZeneca→SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF→CHEMBL3353410. `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` retain the target/company. The same-item `osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab` now correctly refuses both drugs. [HTTP](m5_http.json), [m4 replay](attack_http.json).
- Tested missed separators include newline/tab, slash/pipe, bullets, Arabic comma/semicolon, halfwidth ideographic comma, full stops and phrases such as `as well as`. When they leave multiple drugs in one item and the qualifier is detected, the resulting whole-item refusal is **not reported as should-fix**. The attached-suffix leak is distinct: it accepts the forbidden originator. [113 cases](names_m5_attack.json).
- Graph snapshot: 7 retired nodes, 142 retired edges, no live edges with retired endpoints and no self-loops; all six node types covered. Trial phase/status/start/title/url, sponsor count/class, Drug global count/max phase, action and association score match stored values. PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES, ACTS_ON and role-aware SPONSORS directions agree with stored endpoints. [Exhaustive evidence](db_validate.json), [snapshot](db_snapshot_facts.json).
- A 1,001-Drug fixture crosses three count batches and returns every item with count/weight 1. Ordinary Disease HTTP returns 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 94.144 ms**. 99: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
100:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
101: 
102:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
103:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
104:     work grows linearly with the item."""
105:     text = " ".join(words)
106:     if T.is_distinct_product(text) or T.is_radiotracer(text):
107:         return True
108:     return any(T.is_distinct_product(w) or T.is_radiotracer(w)
109:                for w in (" ".join(words[i:j]) for i in range(len(words))

136:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
137:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
138:         found: list[dict] = []
139:         seen: set = set()
140:         tokens: list[str] = []
141:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
142:         ci = 0
143:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
144:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
145:                 # a slash or bar glued to a word ("bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib")
146:                 # ends the piece; it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
147:                 t = raw.strip(" ?.,!;:/|")
148:                 if t:
149:                     tokens.append(t)
150:                     clause.append(ci)
151:                 if raw.endswith(("/", "|")) or raw in ("/", "|"):
152:                     ci += 1
153:                 elif raw.startswith(("/", "|")) and t:
154:                     clause[-1] = ci = ci + 1
155:             ci += 1
156: 
157:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
158:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
159:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
160:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
161:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
162:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
163:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
164:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
165:         seg: list[int] = []
166:         n = 0
167:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
168:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS:
169:                 n += 1
170:                 seg.append(-1)
171:                 continue
172:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
173:                 n += 1
174:             seg.append(n)
175:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
176:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
177:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
178:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
179:         prev_end = -1
180:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
181:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
182:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
183:             joined = dangling is not None and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0])) \
184:                 and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
185:             prev_end = idx[-1]
186:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
187:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
188:                 named = any(k in refused and (
189:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
190:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
191:                     for k in idx)
192:                 if not hit and not named:
193:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
194:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
195:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
196:                     dangling = text
197:                     continue
198:                 dangling = None
199:             elif joined and hit:
200:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
201:                 dangling = None
202:             else:
203:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
204:                 continue
205:             refused.update(hit)
206:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
207:                           "matched_text": text,
208:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
209:                                  "the originator molecule"})
210: 
211:         matched: set[int] = set()
212:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
213:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
214:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
215:                     continue
216:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
217:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
218:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
219:                 if len(window) < 3:
220:                     continue
221:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
222: 
223:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
224:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
225:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
226:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
227:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
228:                     hits = None
229:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
230:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]

235:                                       "matched_text": window,
236:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
237:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
238:                     continue
239: 
240:                 if window.lower() in self.targets:

245:                                       "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
246:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
247:                     continue
248:

257:                                       "also_matching_ids": self.companies[key][1:]})

259:                     continue
260:

270:     def _drug_spans(self, tokens: list[str], skip: set[int], seg: list[int]) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
271:         """Token spans that name exactly one drug, longest first, not overlapping, each
272:         inside one list item."""
273:         spans: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
274:         taken: set[int] = set(skip)
275:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
276:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
277:                 if taken & set(range(i, i + size)) or len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1:
278:                     continue
279:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
280:                 if len(window) < 3:
281:                     continue
282:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(T.canonical_key(window))
283:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
284:                     hits = None
285:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
286:                     spans.append((i, i + size))
287:                     taken.update(range(i, i + size))
288:         return spans

290:     def org_family(self, company_id: str) -> list[str]:
291:         """The organisation plus any companies a curated parent edge links to it."""
292:         ids = {company_id}
293:         for row in self.conn.execute(
294:                 "SELECT dst FROM edge WHERE rel='PARENT_OF' AND src=?"
295:                 " AND retired_at IS NULL", (company_id,)):
296:             ids.add(row["dst"])
297:         return sorted(ids)

src/textrules.py
135: 
136: # A biosimilar, a nanoparticle/liposomal/pegylated reformulation, or a
137: # sponsor-suffixed version is a separate regulatory product from the
138: # originator. The molecule name is the same; the asset is not.
139: RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT = re.compile(
140:     r"\bbio[\s\-]?similar\b"
141:     r"|\bbio[\s\-]?betters?\b"
142:     r"|\bfollow[\s\-]?on\s+biologic\b"
143:     r"|\bnab[\s\-]"
144:     r"|\bnanoparticle\s+albumin[\s\-]?bound\b"
145:     r"|\balbumin[\s\-]?bound\b"
146:     r"|\bnano[\s\-]?particulat\w*\b|\bnanoparticle\s+paclitaxel\b"
147:     r"|\boral\s+azacitidine\b"
148:     r"|\bliposomal\b|\bpegylated\b|\bpeg(?:ylated)?[\s\-]r?h?[a-z]{1,6}\b"
149:     r"|\bgeneric\s+version\b"
150:     r"|\b(?:injection|solution|product)\s+of\s+[a-z]+mab\b",
151:     re.I,
152: )
153: 
154: # "Bevacizumab-Pfizer", "Trastuzumab-dkst": a hyphenated sponsor name or an
155: # FDA four-letter suffix on an INN stem.
156: RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX = re.compile(
157:     r"^[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|tinib|ase|grastim|stim|poetin)\s*[\-–]\s*(?P<suffix>[A-Za-z]{2,12})$", re.I)
158: 
159: # Words that follow a drug name after a hyphen but are not suffixes at all.
160: # "pembrolizumab-free" is an absence, not a product.
161: SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES = {"free", "only", "like", "base", "type", "form", "dose",
162:                      "naive", "based", "alone", "mono", "plus", "arm"}
163: 
164: # Since 2017 the FDA gives a four-letter suffix to *originator* biologics as
165: # well (cemiplimab-rwlc, dostarlimab-gxly, tislelizumab-jsgr), so a suffix on
166: # its own does not mark a biosimilar. Treating every suffix as one blocked the
167: # originators. A four-letter lowercase suffix is a biosimilar only when it is a
168: # known biosimilar suffix; a longer or capitalised suffix is a sponsor name.
169: BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES = {
170:     # bevacizumab
171:     "awwb", "bvzr", "maly", "adcd", "tnjn", "nwgd",
172:     # trastuzumab
173:     "dkst", "pkrb", "dttb", "qyyp", "anns", "strf",
174:     # rituximab
175:     "abbs", "pvvr", "arrx",
176:     # pegfilgrastim / filgrastim
177:     "jmdb", "cbqv", "bmez", "apgf", "fpgk", "pbbk", "sndz", "aafi", "ayow",
178:     # denosumab
179:     "bbdz", "bmwo", "bnht", "dssb", "kyqq", "nxxp",
180: }
181: 
182: # Biosimilar brand names. A brand is not caught by any pattern; these were
183: # found resolving to their originators (Mvasi, Alymsys, Zirabev -> bevacizumab).
184: BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS = {
185:     "mvasi", "zirabev", "alymsys", "vegzelma", "avzivi", "jobevne", "aybintio",
186:     "onbevzi", "oyavas", "abevmy", "byooviz", "kanjinti", "ogivri", "herzuma",
187:     "ontruzant", "trazimera", "hercessi", "zercepac", "truxima", "ruxience",
188:     "riabni", "rixathon", "udenyca", "fulphila", "ziextenzo", "nyvepria",
189:     "fylnetra", "stimufend", "zarxio", "nivestym", "releuko", "granix",
190: }
191: 
192: 
193: # Reformulated products sold under their own name or code. Checked on the
194: # words of the string, so a brand in brackets ("Paclitaxel (Abraxane)") is seen.
195: REFORMULATION_NAMES = {
196:     "abraxane", "abraxis", "doxil", "caelyx", "lipodox", "onivyde", "depocyt",
197:     "nanopac", "liporaxel", "lipusu", "genexol", "onureg", "cc486",
198:     "pazenir", "abi007", "apealea", "dn101", "asentar",
199: }
200: 
201: 
202: def is_distinct_product(text: str) -> bool:
203:     """True when the string names a product that is not the originator."""
204:     t = (text or "").strip()
205:     if not t:
206:         return False
207:     if RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT.search(t):
208:         return True
209:     words = set(re.findall(r"[a-z]+", t.lower()))
210:     words |= {canonical_key(w) for w in re.findall(r"[A-Za-z]+[\s\-]?\d+", t)}
211:     if words & (BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | REFORMULATION_NAMES):
212:         return True
213:     m = RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX.match(t)
214:     if not m:
215:         return False
216:     suffix = m.group("suffix")
217:     if suffix.lower() in SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES:
218:         return False
219:     # an FDA suffix is four letters, written in either case ("-rwlc", "-RWLC");
220:     # a title-case word is a sponsor name ("-Pfizer", "-Teva")
221:     if len(suffix) == 4 and (suffix.islower() or suffix.isupper()):
222:         return suffix.lower() in BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES
223:     return True
224: 
225: 
226: # An originator's FDA suffix carries no information about the molecule, so it
227: # is removed before lookup: "cemiplimab-rwlc" -> "cemiplimab".
228: RE_FDA_SUFFIX = re.compile(
229:     r"^(?P<inn>[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|ase|grastim|stim|poetin))\s*-\s*(?P<sfx>[A-Za-z]{4})$")
230: 
231: 
232: def strip_fda_suffix(text: str) -> str | None:
233:     m = RE_FDA_SUFFIX.match((text or "").strip())
234:     if not m:
235:         return None
236:     sfx = m.group("sfx")
237:     if sfx.lower() in SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES or not (sfx.islower() or sfx.isupper()):
238:         return None
239:     return m.group("inn")
240: 
241: 
242: # "No pembrolizumab", "without bevacizumab": a negated substance is the
243: # absence of an exposure. Accepting it as CONTAINED_ALIAS recorded the
244: # opposite of what the arm says.
245: RE_NEGATED = re.compile(

src/app.py
1: """A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.
2: 
3:     python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765
4: 
5: A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
6: click to move on),
7: then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
8: how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
9: evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
10: claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
11: see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
12: the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
13: OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
14: pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
15: """
16: from __future__ import annotations
17: 
18: import json
19: import sqlite3
20: import sys
21: import threading
22: from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
23: from pathlib import Path
24: 
25: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
26: import config as C
27: import normalize as N
28: import textrules as T
29: from query import OncoKG
30: 
31: PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
32: PORT = 8765
33: 
34: 
35: class Engine:
36:     def __init__(self):
37:         self.kg = OncoKG()
38:         # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
39:         # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
40:         self.kg.conn.close()
41:         self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
42:         self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
43:         self.lock = threading.Lock()
44:         self.dic = self.kg.dic
45:         self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
46:         target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
47:         self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
48: 
49:     def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
50:         out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
51:         for c in out["components"]:
52:             d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
53:             if d:
54:                 c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
55:                              ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
56:                 c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
57:                                            for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
58:                 c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
59:                     "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
60:                     (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
61:                 par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
62:                 if par:
63:                     c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
64:                         "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
65:                         (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
66:             if c.get("candidates"):
67:                 c["candidate_names"] = [
68:                     (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
69:         return out
70: 
71:     def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
72:         """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.
73: 
74:         `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
75:         the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
76:         mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
77:         page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
78:         sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
79:         keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
80:         conn = self.kg.conn
81:         also: list = []
82:         matched = None
83:         row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
84:                            (text,)).fetchone()
85:         if row:
86:             nid = row["id"]
87:         else:
88:             ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
89:             refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
90:             hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
91:             if not hits:
92:                 if refused:
93:                     return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
94:                 return {"not_found": text}
95:             nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
96:             also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
97:             for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
98:                 lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
99:                                    (oid,)).fetchone()
100:                 if lab and oid != nid:
101:                     also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
102:             # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
103:             # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
104:             key = T.canonical_key(text)
105:             for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
106:                 if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
107:                     lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
108:                                        (cid,)).fetchone()
109:                     if lab:
110:                         also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
111:             t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
112:             if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
113:                 also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
114:             also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
115:         node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
116:                             "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
117:         if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
118:             return {"not_found": text}
119:         rows = conn.execute(
120:             "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
121:             "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
122:             "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
123:             "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
124:             (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
125:         drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
126:         trials = {}
127:         for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
128:             chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
129:             trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
130:                 "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
131:                 "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
132:                 f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
133:         groups: dict = {}
134:         for r in rows:
135:             ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
136:             item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
137:             role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
138:             if r["type"] == "Drug":
139:                 item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
140:                 item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
141:             if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
142:                 n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
143:                 # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
144:                 item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
145:                 item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
146:                 item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
147:             elif r["type"] == "Trial":
148:                 item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
149:                 item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
150:             elif r["type"] == "Drug":
151:                 item["weight"] = item["trials"]
152:             elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
153:                 score = ep.get("association_score")
154:                 item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
155:                 item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
156:             else:
157:                 item["weight"] = 0
158:             if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
159:                 item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
160:             if role:
161:                 item["role"] = role
162:             key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
163:             groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
164:         out = []
165:         for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
166:             items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
167:             items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
168:             out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
169:                         "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
170:         out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
171:         props = _obj(node["props"])
172:         return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
173:                            "matched_text": matched,
174:                            "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
175:                            "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
176:                                      ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
177:                                       "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
178:                 "also": also, "groups": out}
179: 
180:     def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
181:         return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
182: 
183:     def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
184:         """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
185:         kg = self.kg
186:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
187:         evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
188:         res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
189:         res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
190:         return res
191: 
192:     def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
193:         """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
194:         kg = self.kg
195:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
196:         intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
197:         evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
198:         checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
199:                                        vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
200:                                        linker=kg.link_entities)
201:         return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
202:                 "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
203:                               "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
204:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence)}
205: 
206: 
207: def _number(v) -> bool:
208:     return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v
209: 
210: 
211: def _obj(raw) -> dict:
212:     """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
213:     try:
214:         v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
215:     except ValueError:
216:         return {}
217:     return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}
218: 
219: 
220: def make_handler(engine: Engine):
221:     class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
222:         def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
223:             self.send_response(code)
224:             self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
225:             self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
226:             self.end_headers()
227:             self.wfile.write(body)
228: 
229:         def do_GET(self):
230:             if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
231:                 self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
232:             else:
233:                 self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")
234: 
235:         def do_POST(self):
236:             try:
237:                 n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
238:                 body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
239:                 text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
240:                 if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
241:                     raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
242:                 if not text:
243:                     raise ValueError("empty input")
244:                 if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
245:                                      "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
246:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
247:                 with engine.lock:
248:                     if self.path == "/api/explore":
249:                         res = engine.explore(text)
250:                     elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
251:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
252:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
253:                         res = engine.ask(text)
254:                     elif self.path == "/api/structured":
255:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
256:                         if not answer:
257:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
258:                         res = engine.structured(text, answer)
259:                     else:
260:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
261:                         if not answer:
262:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
263:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
264:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
265:                                            allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
266:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
267:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
268:                 self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
269:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
270: 
271:         def log_message(self, *a):
272:             pass
273:     return Handler
274: 
275: 
276: def main() -> None:
277:     port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
278:     engine = Engine()
279:     print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
280:     ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()
281: 
282: 
283: if __name__ == "__main__":
284:     main()
